# Atlas claim ledger

AuthorityEffect: None. Generated by `dag/build_atlas.py` from
[`atlas_claim_ledger.json`](atlas_claim_ledger.json); edit the sources, not this file.

Every claim in [`atlas.md`](atlas.md) is a row here, and every row is one node of
[`dag/derivations_dag.json`](../dag/derivations_dag.json). Part I copies the claims of the archived
papers, one row per claim, with the file and line it comes from. Part II lists the derivation
nodes that the repository audit recorded directly in the DAG.

## Correspondence

- Ledger rows: 1347; DAG derivation nodes: 1347. Each row names one node and each node names one row.
- Part I, archive claims: 607. None of them had a DAG node before the atlas; each now has one (`kind: archive_claim`, id equal to its atlas ID), linked to its pinned source file or paper node.
- Part II, audit derivations: 740. Of these, 97 cite an archived paper and 643 concern code, workflows, tests or other repository sources with no archive claim behind them.
- Not derivations, so not rows: 651 `explicit_assumption_or_obligation`, 38 `paper_assessment`, 1553 `pinned_source_file`.
- No archive claim has been matched to an audit derivation. Where an audit node and an archive row state the same result, both rows stay, and the paper column shows that they concern the same paper; a claim-level match needs a reviewer's reading and is not inferred here.

Status is a coarse reading of the source's own status and evidence text: `proved`, `mechanized`, `computed`, `empirical`, `definition`, `imported`, `scope_fence`, `not_measured`, `withdrawn`, `stated`. The source text is kept in `status_text`; the atlas grades nothing.

Source: [`paper_claim_ledger.md`](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper_claim_ledger.md); claims read from [`paper.md`](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md). Claims: 14.

| Atlas ID | Local ID | Claim | Status | Source text on status, evidence and limits | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ATL-P017-001 |  | Channel-independent soundness: if the decoder binds replies to task \(P\) and \(C(P,w)=\mathsf{accept}\Rightarrow R(P,w)\), the set of accepted invalid candidates is empty for every proposal channel. | `proved` | No acceptance-rate or completeness bound follows.; Theorem 1 | [paper.md:98](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L98) |
| ATL-P017-002 |  | On a directed \(k\)-cycle with claims \(c\), \(\operatorname{im}d_0=\ker d_1=\{\sum c'_i=0\}\) and \(\Phi_1(c)=\Gamma_2(c)=\|s\|=\max_{\partial_1z=0,\\|z\\|_\infty\le1}\langle z,c\rangle\), \(s=\sum_i c_i\). | `proved` | Theorem 2 | [paper.md:150](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L150) |
| ATL-P017-003 |  | The non-faithful prompt trees form a two-sided operad ideal \(\mathcal I\); in \(\mathcal O/\mathcal I\) the null tree absorbs on both sides, and a composite is nonzero iff every leaf span is byte-identical to its declared slice. | `proved` | Theorem 3 | [paper.md:214](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L214) |
| ATL-P017-004 |  | Masking logits to the admissible BPE token frontier keeps every prefix viable and every completed word in the target language. | `proved` | Proposition 4 | [paper.md:246](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L246) |
| ATL-P017-005 |  | With a sound and complete Stage-1 guard and conditionally independent retries, the theorem gives the Stage-1 admission probability, conditional Stage-2 precision and expected Stage-2 call savings of guarded retry with null-tree abstention. | `proved` | Rests on a conditional-independence assumption.; Theorem 5 | [paper.md:293](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L293) |
| ATL-P017-006 |  | Omitting the global cycle-sum equation leaves an infinite false-green kernel \(\mathcal K_{\text{false}}(x)\cong\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}\), parameterised by the cycle-sum error. | `proved` | Theorem 6 | [paper.md:347](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L347) |
| ATL-P017-007 |  | For a spanning tree with \(b_1\ge2\) chords the chord-coordinate incidence of the fundamental cycle basis is \(I_{b_1}\), and the partial-guard false-green kernel has rank \(\|V\|-1+b_1\). | `proved` | Theorem 7 | [paper.md:404](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L404) |
| ATL-P017-008 |  | Tree-then-chord staging lowers the maximum per-turn slot arity from \(\|V\|-1+2b_1\) to \(\max(\|V\|-1,b_1)\), e.g. \((13,17,16,21,21)\to(5,7,6,9,7)\) on \(K_{3,3},Q_3,K_5\), Petersen, \(W_7\), and closes the two-stage false-green kernel. | `proved` | Theorem 8 | [paper.md:491](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L491) |
| ATL-P017-009 |  | Discrete Fredholm alternative: \(d\in\operatorname{im}d_0\) iff every chord holonomy \(r_m^*\) vanishes iff \(d=d_0p_T^*\); otherwise a nonzero holonomy witnesses the obstruction. | `proved` | Theorem 9 | [paper.md:549](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L549) |
| ATL-P017-010 |  | The syndrome \(\mathbf r^*=\hat{\mathbf c}^*-\mathbf c\|_{E\setminus T}=S\mathbf c\) decodes verdict, single-edge repair and audit. | `proved` | Theorem 10 | [paper.md:615](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L615) |
| ATL-P017-011 |  | A single tree-edge integration error shifts the whole descendant subtree's potential while edge-local retry sees one local syndrome; path-integrated \(B_T^{-1}\) diagnostics identify the subtree in one step. | `proved` | Theorem 11 | [paper.md:688](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L688) |
| ATL-P017-012 |  | For C011–C020 the set of schema-valid SOLVED replies violating the witness relation is nonempty (infinite for integer or rational fields); independent exact certificate checking closes it. | `proved` | Theorem 12 | [paper.md:758](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L758) |
| ATL-P017-013 |  | Across the 12 mechanism and cohomology trials, guarded staging with null-tree abstention gave 40/40 conditional Stage-2 precision with 0 admitted false SOLVED verdicts in 242 guarded evaluations. | `empirical` | Precision on admitted cases, not solve rate.; Claim hygiene summary | [paper.md:1307](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L1307) |
| ATL-P017-014 |  | qwen-2.5-7b-instruct passed the complete guard on 2/40 hidden C004 cases, 8/60 C006 boards and 0/10 C011–C020 certificates (10/10 abstained). | `empirical` | No general symbolic competence is claimed.; Claim hygiene summary | [paper.md:1317](archive/pfp/017_pfp_proposal_channels_and_internal_interventions/paper.md?plain=1#L1317) |

| ATL-D-PFP-INTERNAL-EFFECT-CLOSURE | `formal_source_review_and_fresh_build` | The recursive Lean operation-tree fold retains child outputs and has no claimed authority effect because every operation in its admitted table is explicitly assigned none; induction closes that finite contract. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C011 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For valid interpreted graph, let r=c-Dphi, D^T y=0, \|y\|<=w. For any psi, y^T c=y^T(c-Dpsi)<=sum w\|c-Dpsi\|. A checked feasible phi with equal primal and dual objectives is therefore globally optimal. This implication requires weak duality only, not trusting the simplex producer or assuming strong duality. Potential checker repeats this core and additionally checks fundamental cycle residuals using the reference graph helper. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C012 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | H0 completeness needs kernel membership plus independence plus dimC0-rankD cardinality. H1 basis requires rank[D\|B]=dimC1 and \|B\|=dimC1-rankD. Gluing Dx=b is direct; nonzero eta^T b with D^T eta=0 proves inconsistency. Checker2 independently recomputes rank and target augmented rank, while checker1 misses H0 cardinality completeness. Combined gate rejects the reproduced incomplete H0 witness. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C013 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For valid finite free chain complex, integer U,Uinv,V,Vinv with inverse identities and U*d*V diagonal with positive divisibility chain certify cokernel invariant factors. Since ker(d_n) is saturated in free C_n (quotient is im(d_n), free), torsion of C_n/im(d_{n+1}) equals torsion H_n. Betti follows rank-nullity. Checker2 recomputes integer invariant factors and field2 ranks; mere Euler equality or F2 inequality would not determine torsion. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C014 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For D1D0=0 and positive diagonalW, imD0 and im(W^-1D1^T) are W-orthogonal. A decomposition with harmonic h in both kernels is unique by positive definiteness. Exact harmonic membership, independence and cardinality E-rankD0-rankD1 establish basis completeness. Checker1 implements these; checker2 compares uniquely recomputed normal-equation parts but does not inspect harmonic_basis. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C015 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For a valid filtered simplicial boundary over F2, upper-unitriangular V preserves the filtration, R=DV changes basis, and unique nonzero column lows yield persistence pairings. Zero R columns not paired as lows give essential classes. Checker2 independently reduces D by sparse XOR. Equal filtration-value births/deaths are retained as index pairs but omitted from value barcodes. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C016 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | A closed product with nonzero pairing against a closed cycle is nonexact. Complete cup-image rank requires bases spanning the input cohomology, an independent image family, and an upper-span certificate for all products. Independent checker instead computes rank(B+products)-rank(B) using its own exact rational cocycle representatives; this can establish the numerical rank without validating submitted basis completeness. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C017 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | Ordered finite-group products certify plaquette nonflatness. A gauge verified on every declared edge proves trivialization. Flatness plus closed generating torus loops with determinant-one winding matrix and nontrivial commuting holonomy certifies the stated global obstruction on a valid lattice. Checker2 uses the faithful left-regular permutation representation L_g L_h=L_gh and independently reconstructs a spanning-tree gauge. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C018 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | Switching gives an upper bound. Each pairwise edge-disjoint odd-negative closed walk contains a negative cycle and requires a distinct deletion, hence packing size is a lower bound. Equality certifies optimality. Exhaustive2^(n-1) enumeration is complete modulo global sign. UNKNOWN certificates establish bounds and local optimality/maximal packing, not global optimality. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C019 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | An exact potential proves extension. A closed cycle with nonzero B pairing proves rational nonextension by annihilating im(d1). An integer modular cycle with nonzero pairing proves integer nonextension. Weighted-L1 optimality additionally requires a primal attaining the feasible dual objective; merely positive dual feasibility proves only a lower bound. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC1120-C020 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For a finite filtered chain complex, Z_r(n,p)={x in F_p C_n: Dx in F_(p-r) C_(n-1)}. The page is Z_r/(Z_(r-1)(n,p-1)+D Z_(r-1)(n+1,p+r-1)). Independent checker explicitly computes these subquotients with XOR bit-vector bases and induced differential ranks modulo the target denominator. Recursion subtracts both outgoing and incoming ranks; E6 stabilizes for the bounded six-column carrier and its degree sums give total homology. |  |  |
| ATL-D-CAA-C01 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | The1076 fixed artifacts supply703 case/example inputs,344 stored negative mutations, four participant answer collections and finite custody/accounting/history controls. Their pinned prior replays are useful regression evidence conditional on the separately reviewed checker implementation; they do not certify all accepted mathematics. |  |  |
| ATL-D-CAA-C02 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | The retained C017 baseline/immune comparison records0/5 accepted cases in each arm. Its fields permit separate token/cost/call-time arithmetic, but missing full answers and provider receipts prevent independent response rescoring or verified live-cost attribution. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC2130-C021 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For n qubits and commuting Hermitian signed Paulis, an independent binary symplectic basis of rank k has 2^k joint eigenvalue characters, each of multiplicity 2^(n-k). Phase-correct decomposition expresses every term in that basis. Enumerating all characters gives the exact rational minimum of H=sum w(I-sP)/2 and its degeneracy. Separately, exact Gaussian-rational Schur elimination of H-EI establishes positive semidefiniteness and nullity. Those two finite implications justify the numerical minimum under the declared Hilbert-space representation. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC2130-C022 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | For independent commuting Pauli generators spanning isotropic S with 1<=dim S<n, logical operators are vectors in S-perp outside S. A candidate logical of weight d plus exhaustive exclusion of every lower weight establishes distance d. Generator independence excludes -I relations, so phases do not change this distance calculation. Search is bounded at one million lower-weight candidates; unavailable search is not a proof. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC2130-C023 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | H, S and CNOT conjugation of the 2n Pauli generators determines the Clifford automorphism and hence unitary up to global phase. Exact gate replay against an admitted symplectic signed tableau certifies a supplied circuit within gate bound. Failure of one synthesis algorithm to meet a bound is not a proof that no shorter circuit exists. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC2130-C024 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | A sequence of exact local complementations on a labelled simple graph is a constructive orbit-membership witness. Cut rank over F2 is invariant under local complementation by elementary row/column transformations, so a subset with different cut ranks proves nonmembership. Equal cut ranks are not sufficient for membership. Exhaustive orbit closure proves nonmembership only when traversal truly exhausts the finite orbit. |  |  |
| ATL-D-AC2130-C025 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | In Q(zeta_m), positive rational vector norm declarations must equal exact inner products; within-basis orthogonality and d*\|<v,w>\|^2=\|\|v\|\|^2\|\|w\|\|^2 across bases establish the finite MUB predicate after normalization. | ATL-D-RQPR-C004 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | Private validated finite-chain clocks support exact interval discrepancy bounds by telescoping and triangle inequality. | ATL-D-RQPR-C005 | `mathematical_or_implementation_claim` | Classical instruments enforce normalized nonnegative outcome maps, distinguish adaptive from nonadaptive continuation, and recompute the nonadaptive forgetting law.
